NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, September 13, 2016 
PRESENT:  Robert K. Black Jr., Supervisor; Jesse Thompson, Vice-Chairman; Jason E. Whiting, Chairman; 
and Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor, were present in person.  Steve Williams, Supervisor, was absent. 
9:08 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation was led by Supervisor Black.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no comments from the public.   
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes:  August 9, 2016 Regular Meeting and September 8, 2016 Special Meeting 
b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 August 2016  c) Constable Reports: Show Low Precinct #5, August 
2016 d) Justice Court Reports:  Holbrook Precinct #1, August; Winslow Precinct #2, August; Snowflake Precinct 
#3, August; Kayenta Precinct #4, August; Show Low Precinct #5, August; and Pinetop -Lakeside Precinct #6, 
August 2016  e) Clerk of Superior Court Report August 2016  f) Juvenile Probation Report August 2016  g) 
Approval of two Back Tax Land Parcel sold over the counter in the amount of $ 1,187.02: APN 105-35-112 and 
APN 403-09-419 h) Approval of reissuance of Stale Dated Check Nos. 476378 in the amount of $37.20, 477203 
in the amount of $19.49, and 489888 in the amount of $71.15 i) Adoption of Resolution Number 16-16 canceling 
the election and appointing the persons listed on Exhibit A who filed nominating petitions or a nomination paper 
for a write-in candidate for Governing Board for Joseph City USD #2, Holbrook USD #3 (Ward 1, 3, 4 and 5), 
Snowflake USD #5, Heber/Overgaard USD #6, Whiteriver USD #20, Cedar USD #25, NAVIT Districts – 
Snowflake USD #5, Heber/Overgaard USD #6, Show Low USD #10, Whiteriver USD #20 and NATIVE District 
– Pinon USD #4 because the number of candidates who filed were equal to or less than the number of position s 
open for each school district j) Approval of State Plan of Operations between the State of Arizona and the Sheriff’s 
Office for transfer of excess Department of Defense property  k) Donation of a 2006 Chevrolet ¾ ton 4 door 
pickup 194,000 miles to Kayenta Fire District l) Letter to Governor Ducey regarding the FY2017-2018 Executive 
budget proposal to include the counties financial priorities:  Supervisor Thompson made a motion to authorize 
the Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda ; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session: CONSENT 
AGENDA:  Vice-Chairman Thompson made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Public Health Services 
District Board of Directors Session ; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving 
the motion. Mary Herring discussed the items.  
a. Memorandum of Agreement with Jefferson Academy of Advanced Learning from October 1, 2016 to 
September 30, 2018: 
b. Memorandum of Agreement with Window Rock Unified School District and Southwest Food E xcellence 
from October 1, 2016 to September 30, 2018:  
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve the items in the consent agenda; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
Vice-Chairman Thompson made a motion  to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 2 -->
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions: Katherine Wright reviewed the 26-item personnel 
action report: Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the Personnel Action Report as presented; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
b. Consideration and  possible approval of the annual contract for professional services to  Veritas Research 
Consulting, represented by Doyel Shamley in the amount of $60,000.00 plus travel expenses: Mary Springer 
discussed the item and introduced Mr. Doyel Shamley. Mr. Shamley addressed the Board. He spoke about his 
background and the programs that his company conducted in Apache County for which they have received 
acclaim. He spoke about the potential in Navajo County.  He spoke about the BLM Restore Arizona Program 
and that Navajo and Apache Counties are in the forefront, with Navajo County being in the lead.  Mr. Shamley 
answered questions from the Board. Mr. Shamley indicated that his resources are different is that the US 
Forest Services is constrained by law for their lands only  and that his network and collaboration brings 
services that they do not have. He spoke about bringing in these new industries allows more timber sales to 
go forward and that another area of benefit is that you get larger seamless treatment  with collaboration with 
those contacts that are extra jurisdictional to each o ther. Chairman Whiting indicated that Apache County 
has appreciated the services Mr. Shamley has provided and have received real results.  Supervisor Thompson 
made a motion  to approve the annual contract for professional services to Veritas Research Consulting, 
represented by Doyel Shamley in the amount of $60,000.00 plus travel expenses ; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
c. Consideration and possible adoption of the Navajo County Emergency Operations Plan : Mary Springer 
discussed the item. She thanked all of the partners , both public and private , and requested approval . 
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to adopt the Navajo County Emer gency Operations Plan; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
d. Financial Update: Bryan Layton gave a financial update to the Board of Supervisors. He indicated that the 
State shared sales tax for August came in right at budget, and that we are approximately $120,000.00 less than 
where we stood last year.  He spoke of identifying $130,000.00 from Administrative Services , including a 
vacancy savings and also restructuring and rethinking roles and responsibilities.  He spoke about working on 
additional reduction strategies and will continue to monitor revenues going forward.  He stated that he is 
encouraged about the conversations with staff.  He spoke about Paige Peter son in Administrative Services  
who spoke with her staff who suggested looking at increasing the amount of electronic transfers versus manual 
checks. He indicated that Judge Michala Ruechel has been asked about why we don’t have debit card readers 
in the Courts and other locations, and that is being looked into. Supervisor Thompson spoke about continuing 
to provide services to the community and our senior adults. He thanked Mr. Layton for his work. Supervisor 
Whitesinger indicated that she is happy to see the slight increase. Supervisor Whiting indicated that we will 
continue to provide services as constitutionally mandated and that he is encouraged by the positivity that we 
continue to have.  
WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY RECREATION IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT :  Board of 
Directors Session:  Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion  to enter into the White Mountain Lakes County 
Recreation Improvement District; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
CONSENT AGENDA:   Ratification of the Advisory Committee’s decision to hire Joh n Laurendau and Pat 
Stephens on a temporary basis for the Labor  Day weekend at $15.00 per hour and further hire Lem Cook with 
Silver Creek Properties Construction & Development LLC to do weed eating at $25.00 per man hour not to exceed 
$250.00: Melissa Buckley discussed the item and requested that the Board of Directors ratify the decision of the 
Advisory Committee. Supervisor Black made a motion to ratify the Advisory Committee’s decision to hire John 
Laurandau and Pat Stephens on a temporary basis for the Labor Day weekend at $15.00 per hour and further hire 
Lem Cook with Silver Creek Properties Construction & Development LLC to do week eating at $25.00 per man

<!-- page 3 -->
hour not to exceed $250.00 ; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger . Vote unanimous approving the 
motion.  
Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to return to regular session ; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
PUBLIC WORKS:   
a. Consideration and possible approval of Contract #B16 -07-014 Supply of Aggregate for Cover Material to 
Brimhall Sand and Rock and Building Materials, Inc., and Hatch Construction & Paving, Inc.: Glenn Kephart, 
P.E., discussed the item and requested approval. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve Contract 
#B16-07-014 for Cover Ma terial to Brimhall Sand and Rock and Building Materials, Inc., and Hatch 
Construction & Paving, Inc.; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session: CONSENT 
AGENDA:  Vice-Chairman Thompson made a motion  to enter into the Navajo County Flood Control 
District Board of Directors Session ; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. Contract signed by County Manager per Board of Supervisors Resolution: Contract for 
Professional Services between Navajo County Flood Control District and J.E. Fuller Hydrology and 
Geomorphology, Inc. : Glenn Kephart discussed the item.  Supervisor Whitesinger  made a motion to 
approve the item in the consent agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving 
the motion.  
Supervisor Black made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
BOARD OF EQUALIZATION:  Supervisor Whitesinger  made a motion to enter into the Board of 
Equalization; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion  
a. Discussion and possible action regarding Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation APN 210 -44-182; 
Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners : Cammy Darris and Chris Frayer were sworn in. Ms. 
Frayer indicated that there has been an agreement reached on this parcel  with the property owners to adjust 
the full cash value price to $400,000.00 with a limited value of $382,976.00. Supervisor Thompson made a 
motion to adjust the full cash value price to $400,000.00 with a limited value of $328,976.00 as to APN 210-
44-182; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Discussion and possible action regarding Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation APN 212-30-015B; 
swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners : Cammy Darris and Chris Frayer were sworn in. Ms. 
Frayer requested the Board of Equalization consider ARS §43-16107(A).  She indicated that the petitioner 
did not attend the hearing nor the scheduled meeting.  She stated that the handout is what the Assessor’s office 
has put together based on equity of like properties . Based on the fact that the petitio ner did not attend the 
hearing or provide information at this time, she stated that the Assessor’s Office is recommending that the full 
cash value remain at $1,354,351.00 with a limited value to remain at $1,341,639.00. Supervisor Whitesinger 
made a motion to approve keeping the full cash value of APN 212-30-015B at $1,354.351.00 with the limited 
value at $1,341.639.00; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
Supervisor Black made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Whitesinger  gave an update from District V. She spoke about the White Mountain R egional 
Transportation Committee and indicated that participation  is dwindling.  She indicated that the Committee is 
looking at how to utilize the dwindling dollars and work with one another in completing small projects.  She 
stated that she has a partners hip meeting with the White Mountain Apache Tribe concerning transportation and

<!-- page 4 -->
spoke about the challenge of providing quality services with less funding.  She spoke about attending the NACOG 
Regional Council meeting.  She spoke about the Head Start program s throughout the County and the White 
Mountain Apache Tribe Fair and Rodeo.  She expressed appreciation for the ingenuity of County employees for 
the rack system of handing out the County bags at the parade.  Supervisor Black gave an update from District I. 
He spoke about community meetings in Pinon and several other meetings that he has attended with Sheriff Clark. 
He spoke of the Red Dog meeting on September 22nd. He spoke of the Southwest Navajo Fair and parade and the 
Western Agency parade next month. Vice-Chairman Thompson gave a report from District II. He spoke about 
a meeting regarding the Winslow shooting involving a young lady and a Winslow Police Officer. He spoke about 
a Stabilization Center in Winslow , as well as one here in Holbrook , and indica ted that he is working with 
Community Bridges, Inc., in Phoenix, who is willing to do whatever they can to help the treatment center of 
Winslow and also working with the Navajo Nation Human Rights Commission. He spoke of the Navajo Hopi 
Relocation in Window Rock regarding help in improving roads  to a new settlement . He stated that he has also 
been working hard in allowing only the categorical exclusion portion of the process and indicated that he spoke 
at NACO regarding this issue , and NACO is in support . He spoke about the canvass of the election.  He spoke 
about the parades in the community and the opportunity to shake hands with the elderly.  James Jayne indicated 
that recognition of Geoff Mullen and Bryan Cook is in order for improving the bag holders for the parades. 
Chairman Whiting reported from District III. He spoke about meetings regarding the Forest Service and 
industry. He spoke about the potential for the industry getting consistent wood supply through a new partnership.  
He expressed appreciation for the Governor’s office and Senator Flake’s office regarding their involvement in the 
forest issues. He reminded everyone that on October 1, the Bread of Life Mission is having their dinner fundraiser. 
Mr. Carlyon indicated that the Bread of Life is doing their annual pie in the face  and that he and Sheriff Clark 
will compete against each other this year.  Chairman Whiting spoke about the Sweet Corn Festival held in 
Taylor over the Labor Day weekend. He s poke of the Holbrook City Council and the grant which was obtained 
for the Historic County Courthouse. He spoke of a meeting in the Valley that he and Mr. Jayne had with legislators 
and indicated that he felt that the County’s concerns were received very well.  He reminded everyone that the 
Navajo County Fair starts this week on Wednesday and ends on Sunday.  
ADJOURN at 10: 00 a.m. Supervisor Black  made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
 
APPROVED:       DATED:  September 27, 2016 
 
For Jason E. Whiting, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
